\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}\right\|_{2},\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq R
.

Let
\kappa=\beta/\mu_{1}
. We assume WLOG that
R\leq\frac{1}{\kappa(P-1)}
. Let
F_{\hat{\alpha}}(x):=\int_{x^{-(P-1)}}^{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}\frac{dz}{1+\kappa z^{-P/(P-1)}}
. Then
F^{\prime}_{\hat{\alpha}}(x)=\frac{(P-1)x^{-P}}{1+\kappa x^{P}}=\frac{P-1}{(1+\kappa x^{P})x^{P}}
. We will use this function to bound norm growth.
Let
g_{\hat{\alpha},c}(t)=\frac{1}{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-\kappa(P-1)c-2\mu_{1}(P-1)t}
. Define
T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)=\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-\kappa(P-1)r-r^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}
.
It is easy to verify that
g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))=r^{P-1}
.

Lemma I.8
.

For any
x\in[\hat{\alpha},R]
we have

\left(\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-x^{-(P-1)}\right)-F_{\hat{\alpha}}(x)\in[0,\kappa(P-1)x].

Proof.

On the one hand, we have

\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-x^{-(P-1)}-F_{\hat{\alpha}}(x)=\int_{x^{-(P-1)}}^{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}\left(1-\frac{1}{1+\kappa z^{-P/(P-1)}}\right)dz\geq 0.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-x^{-(P-1)}-F_{\hat{\alpha}}(x)=\int_{x^{-(P-1)}}^{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}\frac{\kappa}{z^{P/(P-1)}+\kappa}dz

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\int_{x^{-(P-1)}}^{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}\frac{1}{z^{P/(P-1)}}dz

\displaystyle=\kappa(P-1)\cdot\left.\frac{-1}{z^{1/(P-1)}}\right|_{x^{-(P-1)}}^{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa(P-1)x,

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma I.9
.

Let
{\bm{M}}_{0}
be a PSD matrix with
\|{\bm{M}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq 1
. For
{\bm{M}}(t):={\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{M}}_{0},t)
and
t\leq T_{\hat{\alpha}}(c)
,

\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}=\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(t))\leq g_{\hat{\alpha},c}(t)^{\frac{1}{P-1}}.

Proof.

Since
\|\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})\|_{2}\leq\|\nabla f({\bm{0}})\|_{2}+\beta\|{\bm{M}}\|_{2}^{P}\leq\mu_{1}+\beta(\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}))^{P}
, by
Lemma

I.7
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(0))+\int_{0}^{t}2(\mu_{1}+\beta(\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(\tau)))^{P})(\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(\tau)))^{P}d\tau

\displaystyle=\hat{\alpha}+2\mu_{1}(P-1)\int_{0}^{t}\frac{d\tau}{F_{\hat{\alpha}}^{\prime}(\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(\tau))}

So

F_{\hat{\alpha}}(\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}(t)))\leq 2\mu_{1}(P-1)t.

If
\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}<\hat{\alpha}
, then
\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}\leq g_{\hat{\alpha},c}(t)^{\frac{1}{P-1}}
. If
\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}\geq\hat{\alpha}
, then by
Lemma

I.8
,

F_{\hat{\alpha}}(\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2})\leq 2\mu_{1}(P-1)T_{\hat{\alpha}}(c)=\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-\kappa(P-1)c-c^{-(P-1)}\leq F_{\hat{\alpha}}(c),

so
\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}\leq c
for all
t\leq T_{\hat{\alpha}}(c)
. Applying
Lemma

I.8
again, we have

\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}^{-(P-1)}\leq F(\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2})+\kappa(P-1)c\leq 2\mu_{1}(P-1)t+\kappa(P-1)c,

which implies
\|{\bm{M}}(t)\|_{2}\leq g_{\hat{\alpha},c}(t)^{\frac{1}{P-1}}
by definition.
∎

Consider the following ODE:

\frac{d\widehat{{\bm{M}}}}{dt}=-\left(\nabla f({\bm{0}})\widehat{{\bm{M}}}^{P}+\widehat{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\nabla f({\bm{0}})\right).

We use
\hat{\phi}_{m}(\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0},t)
to denote the solution of
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t)
when
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(0)=\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0}
. For diagonal matrix
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0}
,
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t)
is also diagonal for any
t
, and it is easy to show that

{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t){\bm{e}}_{i}=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{1}{\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0}{\bm{e}}_{i}\right)^{-(P-1)}-2\mu_{i}(P-1)t}\right)^{\frac{1}{P-1}}&\qquad{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0}{\bm{e}}_{i}\neq 0,\\
0&\qquad{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{{\bm{M}}}_{0}{\bm{e}}_{i}=0.\end{cases}

(36)

Remark I.10
.

Unlike depth-2 case, the closed form solution,
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t)
is only tractable for diagonal initialization, i.e., (
36
) (note that the identity matrix is diagonal). And this is the main barrier for extending our two-phase analysis to the case of general initialization when
L\geq 3
. In
Appendix

J
, we give a more detailed discussion on this barrier.

The following lemma shows that the trajectory of
{\bm{M}}(t)
is close to
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t)
.

Lemma I.11
.

Let
{\bm{M}}_{0}
be a diagonal PSD matrix with
\|{\bm{M}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq 1
. For
{\bm{M}}(t):={\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{M}}_{0},t)
and
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(t):=\hat{\phi}_{m}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{M}}_{0},t)
, we have

\|{\bm{M}}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))-\widehat{{\bm{M}}}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(r^{P+1}).

Proof.

We bound the difference
{\bm{D}}:={\bm{M}}-\widehat{{\bm{M}}}
between
{\bm{M}}
and
\widehat{{\bm{M}}}
.

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{D}}}{\textup{{d}}t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=2\left\|\nabla f({\bm{0}})\left({\bm{M}}^{P}-\widehat{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\right)+\left(\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})-\nabla f({\bm{0}})\right){\bm{M}}^{P}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|\nabla f({\bm{0}})\|_{2}\|{\bm{M}}^{P}-\widehat{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})-\nabla f({\bm{0}})\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\bm{M}}^{P}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mu_{1}P\max\{\|{\bm{M}}\|_{2}^{P-1},\|\widehat{{\bm{M}}}\|_{2}^{P-1}\}\|{\bm{D}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\beta\left\|{\bm{M}}\right\|_{2}^{2P}\right),

where the last step is by
Lemma

I.4
. This implies that

\|{\bm{D}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\int_{\tau=0}^{t}\left\|\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{D}}(\tau)}{\textup{{d}}\tau}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\textup{{d}}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{t}2\left(\mu_{1}Pg_{\hat{\alpha},r}(\tau)\left\|{\bm{D}}(\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\beta g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(\tau)^{\frac{2P}{P-1}}\right)d\tau.

So

\displaystyle\|{\bm{D}}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)}2\beta g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)^{\frac{2P}{P-1}}\exp\left(2\mu_{1}P\int_{t}^{T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)}g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(\tau)d\tau\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)}2\beta g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)^{\frac{2P}{P-1}}\exp\left(\frac{P}{P-1}\ln\frac{g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))}{g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)}\right)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)}2\beta g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)^{\frac{P}{P-1}}g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))^{\frac{P}{P-1}}dt

\displaystyle=2\beta\cdot\frac{1}{2\mu_{1}}g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))^{\frac{1}{P-1}}\cdot g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))^{\frac{P}{P-1}}

\displaystyle=\kappa g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))^{\frac{P+1}{P-1}}

\displaystyle=\kappa r^{P+1}.

which proves the bound.
∎

Lemma I.12
.

Let
{\bm{M}}(t)={\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{M}}_{0},t),\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}(t)={\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}_{0},t)
. If
\max\{\|{\bm{M}}_{0}\|_{2},\|\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}_{0}\|_{2}\}\leq 1
. For
t\leq T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)
, we have

\|{\bm{M}}(t)-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}(t)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left(\frac{r}{\hat{\alpha}}\right)^{P}e^{2\kappa r^{P}}\|{\bm{M}}(0)-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}(0)\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Proof.

Define
{\bm{D}}(t)={\bm{M}}(t)-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}(t)
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{D}}}{\textup{{d}}t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=2\left\|\left(\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})\left({\bm{M}}^{P}-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\right)+\left(\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})-\nabla f(\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P})\right)\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\|\nabla f({\bm{M}}^{P})\|_{2}\|{\bm{M}}^{P}-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\beta\|{\bm{M}}^{P}-\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}^{P}\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mu_{1}+\beta\|\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}\|_{2}^{P}+\beta\|{{\bm{M}}}\|_{2}^{P}\right)P\max\{\|{\bm{M}}\|_{2}^{P-1},\|\widetilde{{\bm{M}}}\|_{2}^{P-1}\}\|{\bm{D}}\|_{\mathrm{F}},

where the last step is by
Lemma

I.4
. So

\displaystyle\|{\bm{D}}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{D}}(0)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\exp\left(2P\mu_{1}\int_{0}^{T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r)}\left(1+2\kappa g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)^{\frac{P}{P-1}}\right)g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(t)dt\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{D}}(0)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{P}{P-1}\ln\frac{g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))}{g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(0)}+2\kappa g_{\hat{\alpha},r}(T_{\hat{\alpha}}(r))^{\frac{P}{P-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{D}}(0)\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left(\frac{r}{\hat{\alpha}}\right)^{P}e^{2\kappa r^{P}},

which proves the bound.
∎

Let
{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}(t):={\phi_{m}}\left(\alpha{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)
. Let
\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t):=\lim\limits_{\alpha\to 0}{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\alpha}(t)
.

Lemma I.13
.

For every
t\in(-\infty,+\infty)
,
\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)
exists and
{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)
converges to
\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)
in the following rate:

\left\|{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)-\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(\hat{\alpha}).

Proof.

Let
c
be a sufficiently small constant. Let
\bar{T}:=\frac{-\kappa(P-1)c-c^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}
. We prove this lemma in the cases of
t\in(-\infty,\bar{T}]
and
t>\bar{T}
respectively.

Case 1.

Fix
t\in(-\infty,\bar{T}]
. Then
\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\leq T_{\hat{\alpha}}(c)
. Let
\tilde{\alpha}
be the unique number such that
\kappa(P-1)\tilde{\alpha}+\tilde{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}=\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}
. Let
\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}<\hat{\alpha}
be an arbitrarily small number. Let
t_{0}:=T_{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}}(\tilde{\alpha})=\frac{(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime})^{-(P-1)}-\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}
. By
Lemma

I.11
and (
36
), we have

\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t_{0})-\hat{\alpha}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t_{0})-\hat{\phi}_{m}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq O(\tilde{\alpha}^{P+1}).

By
Lemma

I.9
,
\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t_{0})\|_{2}\leq\tilde{\alpha}
. Then by
Lemma

I.12
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t_{0}+t)-{\bm{\phi}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left(\frac{c}{\tilde{\alpha}}\right)^{P}e^{2\kappa c^{P}}\cdot O(\tilde{\alpha}^{P+1})=O(\tilde{\alpha})=O(\hat{\alpha}).

This implies that
\{{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)\}
satisfies Cauchy’s criterion for every
t
, and thus the limit
\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)
exists for
t\leq\bar{T}
. The convergence rate can be deduced by taking limits for
\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}\to 0
on both sides.

Case 2.

For
t=\bar{T}+\tau
with
\tau>0
,
{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}},\tau)
is locally Lipschitz with respect to
{\bm{M}}
. So

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)-{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}),\tau)-{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}}(\bar{T}),\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=O(\left\|{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T})-{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}}(\bar{T})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}})

\displaystyle=O(\hat{\alpha}),

which proves the lemma for
t>\bar{T}
.
∎

Theorem I.14
.

For every
t\in(-\infty,+\infty)
, as
\alpha\to 0
, we have:

\left\|{\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)-\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(\hat{\alpha}^{\frac{1}{P+1}}),

(37)

and for any
2\leq k\leq d
,

\lambda_{k}\left({\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right)=O(\hat{\alpha}).

(38)

Proof.

Let
{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t):={\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)
. Again we let
c
be a sufficiently small constant and
\bar{T}:=\frac{-\kappa(P-1)c-c^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}
. We prove in the cases of
t\in(-\infty,\bar{T}]
and
t>\bar{T}
respectively.

Case 1.

Fix
t\in(-\infty,\bar{T}]
. Let
\hat{\alpha}_{1}:=\hat{\alpha}^{\frac{1}{P+1}}
. Let
\tilde{\alpha}_{1}
be the unique number such that
\kappa(P-1)\tilde{\alpha}_{1}+\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{-(P-1)}=\hat{\alpha}_{1}^{-(P-1)}
. Let
t_{0}:=T_{\hat{\alpha}}(\tilde{\alpha}_{1})=\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}-\hat{\alpha}_{1}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},t_{0})-\hat{\alpha}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},t_{0})-\hat{\phi}_{m}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},t_{0})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\hat{\phi}_{m}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},t_{0})-\hat{\alpha}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=O(\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{P+1}+\hat{\alpha})

\displaystyle=O(\hat{\alpha}).

By
Lemma

I.9
,
\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}^{\prime}{\bm{I}},t_{0})\|_{2}\leq\tilde{\alpha}_{1}
. Then by
Lemma

I.12
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)-{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},t_{0}+t)-{\phi_{m}}(\hat{\alpha}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}{\bm{e}}_{1}^{\top},t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{c}{\tilde{\alpha}_{1}}\right)^{P}e^{2\kappa c^{P}}\cdot O(\hat{\alpha})=O(\hat{\alpha}^{\frac{1}{P+1}}).

Combining this with the convergence rate for
{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}(t)
proves the bound (
37
).

For (
38
), by
Lemma

I.7
, we have

\begin{split}\lambda^{1-P}_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}))-\lambda^{1-P}_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t_{0}))&\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{\bar{T}}2(P-1)\left\|\nabla f({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}^{P}(t))\right\|_{2}\textup{{d}}t\\
&\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{\bar{T}}2(P-1)(\mu_{1}+\beta\left\|{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{P}))\textup{{d}}t\\
&\leq-2(P-1)\left(\mu_{1}(t-T_{1})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\cdot g_{\hat{\alpha},c}(t)^{\frac{1}{P-1}}\right).\end{split}

(39)

By
Lemma

I.11
,
\lambda_{1}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}))=\left\|{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T})\right\|_{2}=c+O(c^{P+1})
. For
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}))^{-(P-1)}

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)})-2(P-1)\left(\mu_{1}(\bar{T}-T_{1})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\cdot c\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)})-\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-\frac{P-1}{P+1}}-c^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}-O(c)

\displaystyle\geq\Omega(\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}).

Thus
\lambda_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}))\leq O(\hat{\alpha})
.

Case 2.

For
t=\bar{T}+\tau
with
\tau>0
,
{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}},\tau)
is locally Lipschitz with respect to
{\bm{M}}
. So

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)-{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}),\tau)-{\phi_{m}}({\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}(\bar{T}),\tau)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=O\left(\left\|{\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T})-{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}_{\hat{\alpha}_{1}}(\bar{T})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right)=O(\hat{\alpha}^{\frac{1}{P+1}}),

which proves the bound (
37
).

For (
38
), again by
Lemma

I.7
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\lambda^{1-P}_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}))-\lambda^{1-P}_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}+\tau))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\bar{T}}^{\bar{T}+\tau}2(P-1)\left\|\nabla f({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}^{P}(t))\right\|_{2}\textup{{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\bar{T}}^{\bar{T}+\tau}2(P-1)\left(\beta\left\|{\bm{M}}^{P}_{\hat{\alpha}}(t)-({\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}})^{P}(t)\right\|_{2}+\left\|\nabla f\left(({\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}})^{P}(t)\right)\right\|_{2}\right)\textup{{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\bar{T}}^{\bar{T}+\tau}2(P-1)(O({\hat{\alpha}}^{\frac{1}{1+P}})+\beta\left\|{\bm{M}}^{\mathrm{G}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{P})\textup{{d}}t

\displaystyle\leq O(1).

Thus
\lambda^{1-P}_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}+\tau))=\Omega(\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)})
, that is,
\lambda_{k}({\bm{M}}_{\hat{\alpha}}(\bar{T}+\tau))=O(\hat{\alpha})
,
\forall k\geq 2
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

6.2
.

Note that
\left(\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right)^{P}=\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(t)
and

\left({\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right)^{P}=\phi\left({\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{{\hat{\alpha}}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right).

By
Theorem

I.14
, We have

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\phi\left({\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{{\alpha}^{-(1-1/P)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)-\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left({\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right)^{P}-\left(\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right)^{P}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq P\left\|{\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)-\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\max\left(\left\|{\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right\|_{2},\left\|\overline{{\bm{M}}}(t)\right\|_{2}\right)^{P-1}

\displaystyle=O(\hat{\alpha}^{\frac{1}{P+1}})O(1)=O(\alpha^{\frac{1}{P(P+1)}}),

and for
2\leq k\leq d
,

\lambda_{k}\left({\phi}\left({\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{{\alpha}^{-(1-1/P)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right)=\lambda_{k}\left({\phi_{m}}\left(\hat{\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{\hat{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}(P-1)}+t\right)\right)=O(\hat{\alpha}^{P})=O(\alpha).

∎

Appendix J
Escaping direction for deep matrix factorization

For deep matrix factorization, recall that we only prove that GF with infinitesimal identity initialization escapes in the direction of the top eigenvector. The main burden for us to generalize this proof to general initialization is that we don’t know how to analyze the early phase dynamics of (
12
), i.e., the analytical solution of (
40
) is difficult to compute, when
L\geq 3
. Intuitively, the direction that the infinitesimal initialization escapes
{\bm{0}}
is exactly
\overline{{\bm{M}}}:=\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{{\bm{M}}(t)}{\left\|{\bm{M}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}
, where
{\bm{M}}(t)
is the solution of (
40
). Showing
\overline{{\bm{M}}}={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
is a critical step in our analysis towards convergence to GLRL.

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{M}}}{\textup{{d}}t}=-\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{M}}^{L/2}-{\bm{M}}^{L/2}\nabla f({\bm{0}}).

(40)

However, unlike the depth-2 case,
\overline{{\bm{M}}}
can be different from
{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
even if
{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{M}}(0){\bm{v}}_{1}>0
. We here give an example for diagonal
{\bm{M}}(0)
and
\nabla f({\bm{0}})
at
Section

J.2
.
Nevertheless, we still conjecture that except for a zero measure set of
{\bm{M}}(0)
,
\overline{{\bm{M}}}={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
, based on the following theoretical and experimental evidences:

•

If
{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{M}}(0){\bm{v}}_{1}>0
and
\mathrm{rank}({\bm{M}}(0))=1
, we prove that
\overline{{\bm{M}}}={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
. (See
Theorem

J.1
)

•

For the counter-example, we show experimentally, even with perturbation of only magnitude
10^{-5}
,
\overline{{\bm{M}}}={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
. The results are shown at
Figure

7
. The
y
-axis indicates
\langle{\bm{v}}_{1},{\bm{u}}_{1}(t)\rangle
where
{\bm{u}}_{1}(t)
is the top eigenvector of
{\bm{M}}(t)
. As
\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
becomes larger,
{\bm{u}}_{1}(t)
aligns better with
{\bm{v}}_{1}
, which means the noise helps
{\bm{M}}
escaping from
{\bm{v}}_{1}
. The larger the noise is, the faster
{\bm{u}}_{1}(t)
converges to
{\bm{v}}_{1}
.

J.1
Rank-one Case

Theorem J.1

(rank-1 initialization escapes along the top eigenvector)
.

When
\mathrm{rank}({\bm{M}}(0))=1
,
\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{{\bm{M}}(t)}{\left\|{\bm{M}}(t)\right\|_{F}}={\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
, if
{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{M}}(0){\bm{v}}_{1}>0
.

Proof.

Let
{\bm{u}}(0)
be the vector such that
{\bm{M}}(0)={\bm{u}}(0){\bm{u}}(0)^{\top}
and
{\bm{u}}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the solution of

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{u}}(t)}{\textup{{d}}t}=\left\|{\bm{u}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{L-2}\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{u}}(t).

It is easy to check that
{\bm{M}}(t)={\bm{u}}(t){\bm{u}}(t)^{\top}
is the solution of (
40
), because

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{M}}}{\textup{{d}}t}=\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\textup{{d}}t}{\bm{u}}^{\top}+{\bm{u}}\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\textup{{d}}t}^{\top}=

\displaystyle-\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{M}}(t)\left\|{\bm{u}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{L-2}-{\bm{M}}(t)\nabla f({\bm{0}})\left\|{\bm{u}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{L-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{M}}^{L/2}-{\bm{M}}^{L/2}\nabla f({\bm{0}}).

Let
\tau(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\left\|{\bm{u}}(s)\right\|_{2}^{L-2}\textup{{d}}s
. Then

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\textup{{d}}\tau}=\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{u}}}{\textup{{d}}t}\frac{\textup{{d}}t}{\textup{{d}}\tau}=-\frac{1}{\ \ \frac{\textup{{d}}\tau}{\textup{{d}}t}\ \ }\|{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{L-2}\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{u}}=-\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{u}}.

That is, under time rescaling
t\to\tau(t)
, the trajectory of
{\bm{u}}(t)
still follows the power iteration, regardless of the depth
L
.
∎

J.2
Counter-example for Escaping Direction

Let
\nabla f({\bm{0}})=\mathrm{diag}(2,0.9,0.8,\dots,0.1)\in\mathbb{R}^{10\times 10}
be diagonal. Let
{\bm{W}}(0)
be also diagonal and
{\bm{W}}(0)_{i,i}\sim\text{Unif}[0.9,1.1]\cdot\alpha
for
i\in[10]\setminus\{2\}
,
{\bm{W}}(0)_{2,2}=16\alpha
, where
\alpha=10^{-16}
is a small constant. Let the depth be 4.

Lemma J.2
.

With
\nabla f({\bm{0}})
and
{\bm{W}}(0)
constructed above,
{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{M}}(0){\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}>0
and
\overline{{\bm{M}}}\neq{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
.

Proof.

It is easy to check that
{\bm{v}}_{1}={\bm{e}}_{1}
, so
{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{M}}(0){\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}>0
. Now we prove that
\overline{{\bm{M}}}(\infty)\neq{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
.

As both
{\bm{W}}(0)
and
\nabla f({\bm{0}})
are diagonal,
{\bm{W}}(t)
is always diagonal and has dynamics

\frac{\textup{{d}}M(t)_{i,i}}{\textup{{d}}t}=-2\nabla f({\bm{0}})_{i,i}M(t)_{i,i}^{2},\quad\forall i\in[10],

therefore we have closed form of
{\bm{M}}(t)
:

M(t)_{i,i}^{-1}=M(0)_{i,i}^{-1}-2\nabla f({\bm{0}})_{i,i}t,\quad\forall i\in[10].

For
i\in[10]
, the time for
M(t)_{i,i}
going to infinity is
(2M(0)_{i,i}\nabla f({\bm{0}})_{i,i})^{-1}
. By simple calculation,
M(t)_{2,2}
goes to infinity the fastest, thus
\overline{{\bm{M}}}={\bm{e}}_{2}{\bm{e}}_{2}^{\top}\neq{\bm{v}}_{1}{\bm{v}}_{1}^{\top}
.
∎

Figure 7:
Dynamics of
\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{M}}}{\textup{{d}}t}=-\nabla f({\bm{0}}){\bm{M}}^{L/2}-{\bm{M}}^{L/2}\nabla f({\bm{0}})
plotted, where
L=4
,
{\bm{u}}_{1}(t)
is the top eigenvector of
{\bm{W}}(t)
and
\epsilon
is the relative magnitude of noise. The initialization we use in this experiment is
{\bm{W}}_{\text{noise}}(0)={\bm{W}}(0)+\frac{\alpha\epsilon}{2}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{Z}}^{\top})
, where
{\bm{W}}(0)
is what we construct at
Section

J.2
, and
{\bm{Z}}
is a matrix where entries are i.i.d. samples from the standard Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
We run 5 fixed random seeds (the noise matrix) for each
\epsilon
.
The trajectory of
{\bm{W}}
is calculated by simulating gradient flow on
{\bm{M}}
with small timestep and RMSprop
[
Tieleman and Hinton 2012
]
for faster convergence.

We remark that the scales of
{\bm{W}}(0)
and
\nabla f({\bm{0}})
do not matter as in gradient flow, as scaling
\nabla f({\bm{0}})
is equivalent to scaling time (by
Lemma

J.3
below). And for this reason, the
x
-axis is the chosen as
\frac{\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}
, the relative growth rate.

Lemma J.3
.

Suppose
{\bm{g}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a
P
-homogeneous function, that is,
{\bm{g}}(\alpha{\bm{\theta}})=\lambda^{P}{\bm{g}}(\alpha)
for any
\alpha>0
, and
\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(t)}{\textup{{d}}t}={\bm{g}}({\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(t))
. Then
\alpha{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t)
is the solution of

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{\theta}}(t)}{\textup{{d}}t}={\bm{g}}({\bm{\theta}}(t)),\qquad{\bm{\theta}}(0)=\alpha{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(0).

(41)

Proof.

Simply plug in
{\bm{\theta}}(t)=\alpha{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t)
, then we have

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{\theta}}(t)}{\textup{{d}}t}=\frac{\textup{{d}}\alpha{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t)}{\textup{{d}}t}=\alpha^{P}\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t)}{\textup{{d}}(\alpha^{P-1}t)}=\alpha^{P}{\bm{g}}({\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t))={\bm{g}}(\alpha{\bm{\theta}}^{\prime}(\alpha^{P-1}t))={\bm{g}}({\bm{\theta}}(t)).

∎

Appendix K
Proof of Linear Convergence to Minimizer

In this section, we will present the theorems that guarantee the linear convergence to a minimizer
{\bm{W}}_{0}
of
f(\,\cdot\,)
if the dynamics (
42
) is initialized sufficiently close to
{\bm{W}}_{0}
, i.e.,
\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
is sufficiently small. In
Section

K.3
, we will apply this result to prove
Theorem

6.4
.

\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{W}}}{\textup{{d}}t}=-\sum_{i=0}^{L-1}{\bm{W}}^{\frac{2i}{L}}\nabla f({\bm{W}}){\bm{W}}^{2-\frac{2i+2}{L}}=:{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}).

(42)

Throughout this section, we assume
\mathrm{rank}({\bm{W}}_{0})=k
and use
m:=\lambda_{k}({\bm{W}}_{0})
to denote the
k
-th smallest non-zero eigenvalue of
{\bm{W}}_{0}
. The tangent space of manifold of rank-
k
symmetric matrices at
{\bm{W}}_{0}
is
{\mathcal{T}}=\{{\bm{V}}{\bm{W}}_{0}^{\top}+{\bm{W}}_{0}{\bm{V}}^{\top}:{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}\}
. It can be shown that
\dim({\mathcal{T}})=k(d-k)+\frac{k(k+1)}{2}=\frac{k(2d-k+1)}{2}
.

Let
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}})
be the Jacobian of
{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}})
in (
42
). For depth-2 case, we have shown that
{\mathcal{T}}
is an invariant subspace of
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})
in
Theorem

F.5
, property 2. This can be generalize to the deep case where
L\geq 3
. Therefore, we can use
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
to denote the linear operator
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})
restricted on
{\mathcal{T}}
. We also define
\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}({\bm{W}})
as the projection of
{\bm{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
on
{\mathcal{T}}
, and
\Pi^{d^{2}}_{2}({\bm{W}}):={\bm{W}}-\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}({\bm{W}})
.

Towards showing the main convergence result in the section, we make the following assumption.

Assumption K.1
.

Suppose
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}
diagonalizable and all eigenvalues are negative real numbers.

{\bm{W}}_{0}
is a minimizer, so it is clear that
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}
has no eigenvalues with positive real parts (otherwise there is a descending direction of
f(\,\cdot\,)
from
{\bm{W}}_{0}
, since the loss
f(\,\cdot\,)
strictly decreases along the trajectory of (
42
)). If further
K.1
holds, then we know
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}:{\mathcal{T}}\to{\mathcal{T}}
can be diagonalized as
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}[\,\cdot\,]={\mathcal{V}}(\Sigma{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}(\,\cdot\,))
, where
{\bm{\Sigma}}_{i}=\mathrm{diag}(-\mu_{1},\ldots,-\mu_{\dim({\mathcal{T}})})
,
{\mathcal{V}}:\mathbb{R}^{\dim({\mathcal{T}})}\to{\mathcal{T}}
,
{\mathcal{V}}({\bm{x}})=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim({\mathcal{T}})}x_{i}{\bm{V}}_{i}
, and
{\bm{V}}_{i}
is the eigenvector associated with eigenvalue
-\mu_{i}
.

As shown in
Theorem

K.3
below, this assumption implies that if
{\bm{W}}(0)
is rank-
k
and is sufficiently close to
{\bm{W}}_{0}
, then
\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{F}\leq Ce^{-\mu_{1}t}
for some constant
C
. For depth-2 case, the above assumption is equivalent to that
\mathcal{L}({\bm{U}}_{0})
is “strongly convex” at
{\bm{U}}_{0}
, except those 0 eigenvalues due to symmetry, by property 2 of
Theorem

F.5
). For the case where
L\geq 3
, because this dynamics is not gradient flow, in general it does not correspond to a loss function and strongly convexity does not make any sense. Nevertheless, in experiments we do observe linear convergence to
{\bm{W}}_{0}
, so this assumption is reasonable.

K.1
Rank-
k
Initialization

For convenience, we define for all
{\bm{W}}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{d}
,

\left\|{\bm{W}}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}:=\left\|{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left(\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{W}}\right)\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},\quad\left\|{\bm{W}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}:=\left\|\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{W}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},\quad\left\|{\bm{W}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}:=\left\|\Pi^{d^{2}}_{2}\left({\bm{W}}\right)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

The reason for such definition of norms, as we will see later, is that the norm (or the difference) in the tangent space of the manifold of symmetric rank-
r
matrices,
\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}
, dominates that in the orthogonal complement of the tangent space,
\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}
, when both
{\bm{W}},{\bm{W}}^{\prime}
get very close to the
{\bm{W}}_{0}
(see a more rigorous statement in
Lemma

K.2
). WLOG, we can assume

\frac{\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}}{K}\leq\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1},

for some constant
K
, which may depend on
f
and
{\bm{W}}_{0}
. This also implies that
\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\left\|\,\cdot\,\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. Below we also assume for sufficiently small
R
, and any
{\bm{W}}
such that
\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq R
, we have
\left\|\nabla f({\bm{W}})\right\|_{2}\leq\rho
and
\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}})[{\bm{\Delta}}]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\beta\left\|{\bm{\Delta}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
for any
{\bm{\Delta}}
. In the proof below, we assume such properties hold as long as we can show the boundedness of
{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}
.

Lemma K.2
.

Let
\max\{\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1},\left\|{\bm{W}}^{\prime}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\}=r
, when
r\leq\frac{m}{2}
, we have

\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}\leq\frac{5r}{m}\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}.

As a special case, we have

\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}\leq\frac{5\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}^{2}}{m}.

Proof.

WLOG we can assume
{\bm{W}}_{0}
is only non-zero in the first
k
dimension, i.e.,
[{\bm{W}}_{0}]_{ij}=0
, for all
i\geq k+1
,
j\geq k+1
. We further denote
{\bm{W}}
and
{\bm{W}}^{\prime}
by

{\bm{W}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{A}}&{\bm{B}}^{\top}\\
{\bm{B}}&{\bm{C}}\end{bmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad{\bm{W}}^{\prime}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{A}}^{\prime}&{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\\
{\bm{B}}^{\prime}&{\bm{C}}^{\prime}\end{bmatrix},

where
{\bm{A}},{\bm{A}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},{\bm{B}},{\bm{B}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d-k)\times k},{\bm{C}},{\bm{C}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d-k)\times(d-k)}
. By definition, we have
\left\|{\bm{A}}-{\bm{A}}^{\prime}\right\|_{F},\left\|{\bm{B}}-{\bm{B}}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}
and
\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}=\left\|{\bm{C}}-{\bm{C}}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}
. Moreover, we have
\lambda_{\min}({\bm{A}})\geq m-\left\|{\bm{A}}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{F}\geq m-\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\geq\frac{m}{2}
.

Since
{\bm{W}},{\bm{W}}^{\prime}
is rank-
k
, we have
{\bm{C}}={\bm{B}}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{\top},{\bm{C}}^{\prime}={\bm{B}}^{\prime}{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}^{-1}{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}^{\top}
. Thus

\displaystyle\quad\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{C}}-{\bm{C}}^{\prime}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{B}}{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{\top}-{\bm{B}}^{\prime}{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}^{-1}{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{B}}-{\bm{B}}^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\bm{A}}^{-1}{\bm{B}}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|{\bm{B}}{\bm{A}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\bm{A}}^{\prime}-{\bm{A}}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}^{-1}{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\|{\bm{B}}^{\prime}{{\bm{A}}^{\prime}}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|{\bm{B}}^{\top}-{{\bm{B}}^{\prime}}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\frac{2r}{m}+\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\left(\frac{2r}{m}\right)^{2}+\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\frac{2r}{m}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}-{\bm{W}}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\frac{5r}{m}.

∎

Theorem K.3

(Linear convergence of rank-
k
matrices)
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{rank}({\bm{W}}(0))=\mathrm{rank}({\bm{W}}_{0})=k
and

\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq R:=\max\left\{\frac{m}{2K},\frac{\mu_{1}}{K^{2}(29\beta+10\rho/m)}\right\},

we have
\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq Ce^{-\mu_{1}t}\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
for some constant
C
depending on
{\bm{W}}_{0}
, where
{\bm{W}}(t)
satisfies (
42
).

Proof.

For convenience, we define
{\bm{W}}_{1}(t):=\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right),{\bm{W}}_{2}(t):=\Pi^{d^{2}}_{2}\left({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right)=\Pi^{d^{2}}_{2}\left({\bm{W}}(t)\right)
. We also use
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}=\left<{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left(\cdot\right),{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left(\cdot\right)\right>
for short.

\displaystyle\frac{\textup{{d}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{\textup{{d}}t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\textup{{d}}\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{\textup{{d}}t}

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left(\frac{\textup{{d}}{\bm{W}}(t)}{\textup{{d}}t}\right),\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t))\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left\|{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0})-{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)+{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)]\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left\|{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0})-{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)]\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}+2\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left\|{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0})-{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}.

For the first term
\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)]\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}
, we know
{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\in{\mathcal{T}}
, and
{\mathcal{T}}
is an invariant space of
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})
. Recall
{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\lvert_{\mathcal{T}}[\cdot]={\mathcal{V}}\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left(\cdot\right)\right)
, we have

2\left\langle\Pi^{d^{2}}_{1}\left({\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)]\right),{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\rangle_{{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}}=2\left<\Sigma{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left({\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right),{\mathcal{V}}^{-1}\left({\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right)\right>\leq-2\mu_{1}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}.

For the second term
2\beta\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
, we have

2\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq 2\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)]\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\left\|{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=2\rho\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

For the third term
2\left\|{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0})-{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
, we have

\displaystyle 2\left\|{\bm{g}}({\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0})-{\bm{J}}({\bm{W}}_{0})[{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}]\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\beta(\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|^{2}_{F}+\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq 4\beta(K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}).

Thus we have shown the following. Note so far we have not used the assumption that
{\bm{W}}
is rank-
k
.

\frac{\textup{{d}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{\textup{{d}}t}\leq-2\mu_{1}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\left(\rho\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+2\beta K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+2\beta\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right),

that is,

\frac{\textup{{d}}\log\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{\textup{{d}}t}\leq-2\mu_{1}+4\beta K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\frac{4\beta\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\rho\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}}.

(43)

Let
T:=\sup\{t\geq 0:\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\frac{m}{2K}\}
. Setting
{\bm{W}}^{\prime}={\bm{W}}_{0}
in
Lemma

K.2
, we have for
t<T
,
r=\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq K\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\frac{m}{2}
.
Thus,

\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}\leq\frac{5\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}^{2}}{m}\leq\frac{5K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{m}\leq\frac{5}{4}m.

Thus, from (
43
) we can derive that

\frac{\textup{{d}}\log\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}}{\textup{{d}}t}\leq-2\mu_{1}+K^{2}(29\beta+10\rho/m))\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq-\mu_{1}.

(44)

Since
\mu_{1}<0
,
\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
decreases for
[0,T)
. Thus
T
must be
\infty
, otherwise
\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(T)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\lim_{t\to T^{-}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}<R_{1}
. Contradiction.

Therefore, for any
t\in[0,\infty)
, we have
\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}e^{-\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}t}
. That is,

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\textup{{d}}t\leq\frac{2}{\mu_{1}}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\frac{2R}{\mu_{1}}.

Thus from (
44
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\exp\left(-\mu_{1}t+\frac{K^{2}}{2}(29\beta+10\rho/m)\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\textup{{d}}t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\exp\left(-\mu_{1}t+\frac{K^{2}R}{\mu_{1}}(29\beta+10\rho/m)\right)

\displaystyle=:C\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}e^{-\mu_{1}t},

which completes the proof.
∎

K.2
Almost Rank-
k
Initialization

We use
{\bm{M}}(t)
to denote the top-
k
components of
{\bm{W}}(t)
in SVD, and
{\bm{N}}(t)
to denote the rest part, i.e.,
{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{M}}(t)
. One can think
{\bm{M}}(t)
as the main part and
{\bm{N}}(t)
as the negligible part.

Below we show that for deep overparametrized matrix factorization, where
{\bm{W}}(t)
satisfies (
42
), if the trajectory is initialized at some
{\bm{W}}(0)
in a small neighborhood of the
k
-th critical point
{\bm{W}}_{0}
of deep GLRL, and
{\bm{W}}(0)
is approximately rank-
k
, in the sense that
{\bm{N}}(0)
is very small, then
\inf_{t\geq 0}\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
is roughly at the same magnitude of
{\bm{N}}(0)
.

Theorem K.4

(Linear convergence of almost rank-
k
matrices, deep case)
.

Suppose
{\bm{W}}_{0}
is a critical point of rank
k
and
{\bm{W}}_{0}
satisfies
K.1
, there exists constants
C_{0}
and
r
, such that if
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq r
, then there exists a time
T
and constants
C,C^{\prime}
, such that

(1).

\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq Ce^{-\mu_{1}t/2}\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
, for
t\leq T
.

(2).

\left\|{\bm{W}}(T)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq C^{\prime}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
.

Proof.

When
\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}{4}
,
\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}{4}
, thus we have

\left\|{\bm{M}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}{2},

thus by
Lemma

K.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{M}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5\left\|{\bm{M}}(t)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}^{2}}{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}^{2}+10\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{10K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}^{2}+10\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}{{\lambda_{\min}}({\bm{W}}_{0})}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}.

Thus we can pick constant
C_{0}
large enough and
r
small enough, such that for any
t\geq 0
, if
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq r
, then it holds that:

•

The “small terms” in the RHS of (
43
) satisfies that

4\beta K^{2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\frac{4\beta\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\rho\left\|{\bm{W}}_{2}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}{\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}}\leq C_{1}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}+C_{2}\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\mu_{1}

for some
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
independent of
t
.

•

The spectral norm
\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla f({\bm{W}}(t))\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\nabla f({\bm{W}}_{0})\right\|_{2}=:\rho
for all
t\geq 0
.

•

\forall x<r
,
\frac{\kappa_{L}x^{\frac{2}{L}-1}}{(L-2)\rho}>\frac{2}{\mu_{1}}\ln\frac{2r}{C_{0}x}
, where
\kappa_{L}=1-0.5^{\frac{L-2}{L}}
.

Note these conditions can always be satisfied by some
C_{0}
and
r
because we can first find 3 groups
(C_{0},r)
to satisfy each individual condition, and then take the maximal
C_{0}
and minimal
r
, it’s easy to check these conditions are still verified.
And we let
T_{C_{0},r}
be the earliest time that such condition, i.e.,
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq r
fails. Thus by (
43
), for
t\in[0,T_{C_{0},r})
, we have
\left\|{\bm{W}}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}=\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}e^{-\frac{\mu_{1}t}{2}}=\left\|{\bm{W}}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}e^{-\frac{\mu_{1}t}{2}}
. Thus (1) holds for any
T
smaller than
T_{C_{0},r}
. If
T_{C_{0},r}=\infty
, then clearly we can pick a sufficiently large
T
, such that (2) holds. Therefore, below it suffices to consider the case where
T_{C_{0},r}
is finite. And we know the condition that fails must be
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(t)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
, i.e.
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(T_{C_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(T_{C_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}
.

By (
35
) in
Lemma

I.7
, we have

\left\lvert\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}-\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}\right\rvert\leq(L-2)\rho t.

Define
T^{\prime}:=\frac{\kappa_{L}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}}{(L-2)\rho}
, we know for any
t<T^{\prime}
, we have
\left\lvert\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}-\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}\right\rvert\leq\kappa_{L}\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}
.
That is,

\frac{\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}}{{\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}}}\in\left[1-\kappa_{L},\frac{1}{1-\kappa_{L}}\right]=\left[0.5^{\frac{L-2}{L}},0.5^{-\frac{L-2}{L}}\right]\Longrightarrow\frac{\left\|{\bm{N}}(t)\right\|_{2}}{{\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}}}\in[1/2,2].

Now we claim it must hold that
T^{\prime}\geq T_{C_{0},r}
. Otherwise, we have

\frac{C_{0}}{2}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}\leq C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(T^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(T^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq e^{-\mu_{1}T^{\prime}/2}\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq e^{-\mu_{1}T^{\prime}/2}r.

Therefore,
\frac{\kappa_{L}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}^{\frac{2}{L}-1}}{(L-2)\rho}=T^{\prime}\leq\frac{2}{\mu_{1}}\ln\frac{2r}{C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}}
, which contradicts to the definition of
C_{0}
and
r
.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle 2C_{0}\sqrt{d}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{2}\geq 2C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(T_{c_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(T_{C_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle\geq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}e^{-\mu_{1}T_{C_{0},r}/2},

and therefore,

T_{C_{0},r}\leq\frac{2}{\mu_{1}}\ln\frac{\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}}{2\sqrt{d}C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}.

Thus by
Lemma

K.2
, we know

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{W}}(T_{C_{0},r})-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}(T_{C_{0},r})-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},1}+\left\|{\bm{W}}(T_{C_{0},r})-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq K\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(T_{C_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}+\left\|{\bm{M}}(T_{C_{0},r})-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}+\left\|{\bm{N}}(T_{C_{0},r})\right\|_{\mathrm{F},2}

\displaystyle\leq O(\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}})+O(\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2})+O(\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}})

\displaystyle=O(\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}).

∎

K.3
Proof for
Theorem

6.4

Proof for
Theorem

6.4
.

Let
C_{0},r
be the constants predicted by
Theorem

K.4
w.r.t. to
\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(\infty)
.
We claim that we can pick large enough constant
T
, and
\alpha_{0}
sufficiently small, such that for all
\alpha\leq\alpha_{0}
, the initial condition in
Theorem

K.4
holds, i.e.
C_{0}\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|{\bm{W}}_{1}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq r
, where
{\bm{W}}(0):=\phi\left({\alpha}{\bm{I}},\frac{{\alpha}^{-(P-1)}}{2\mu_{1}^{-1}(P-1)}+T\right)
.

This is because we can first ensure
\left\|\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(T)-\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(\infty)\right\|_{2}
is sufficiently small, i.e., smaller than
\frac{r}{2}
. By
Theorem

6.2
, we know when
\alpha\to 0
,
\left\|\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(T)-{\bm{W}}(0)\right\|_{\mathcal{V}}\leq K\left\|\overline{{}{\bm{W}}}(T)-{\bm{W}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=o(1)
and
\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(\alpha)
.

By
Theorem

K.4
, we know there is a time
T
(either
T_{C_{0},r}
or some sufficiently large number when
T_{C_{0},r}=\infty
), such that
\left\|{\bm{W}}(T)-{\bm{W}}_{0}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}=O(\left\|{\bm{N}}(0)\right\|_{\mathrm{F}})=O(\alpha)
.
∎
